## 8 VERDICT for the script

**Does Popeyes have a documented, dated "price paid" in Canada (2022–2026)? — STRONG, with a caveat on scope.**

Strongest items (all tier (a)/(b), dated):

1. **Franchisee collapse and closures, Sep–Oct 2025.** RBI terminated 13 GTA franchise agreements (29 Sep 2025, Globe); the Ontario Superior Court (Commercial List) placed 14 Popeyes franchisee corporations into interim receivership on 10 Oct 2025 with ~$10.83M owed to Meridian Credit Union plus unpaid wages, source deductions and HST (Globe 23 Oct 2025; Insolvency Insider 2 Nov 2025). This meets two of the brief's tests: franchisee insolvency and store closures. Label: court order / franchisor action; the wage and sourcing claims against the franchisee remain allegations.
2. **Pending $35M+ lawsuit naming the franchisor, RBI and its Canadian supply co-op** — ADP Direct Poultry Ltd. v. Popeyes Louisiana Kitchen Inc. et al., Ontario Superior Court, issued 26 May 2025, defendants added Nov 2025, procedural ruling 2026 ONSC 1823. Label strictly: **allegation, pending, untested; RBI denies and says it "found no evidence."**
3. **Company-disclosed sales decline in the segment that contains Canada.** PLK (U.S. & Canada) comparable sales: +0.4% FY2024 → −3.2% FY2025 → −6.5% Q1 2026 → −5.1% Q2 2026; RBI CEO said franchisee profitability fell to "roughly $235,000" and Popeyes' president was replaced (Nov 2025). Caveat: RBI labels these comps "U.S." and publishes no Canada-only figure, so the script must say "the U.S.-and-Canada segment", not "Canada".

What is **not** documented: any net decline in Canadian Popeyes units (company figures rose 230 → 300 → 380 from 2020 to 2025); any Canadian price increase or rollback at tier (a)/(b); any Competition Bureau matter; any Canadian class action; any exit from a province; any franchisee suit against the franchisor. The Jan 2025 $4 value menu is a company value launch, not a documented reversal.

---

## 9 UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

1. **blogTO items** — Parkdale/1402 Queen St. W. "Notice of Distress"/$14,000 rent (Apr 2026); Bloor St. closure/reopening (Jan 2024); Christie Pits "cursed location" lockout (Sep 2022); Scarborough protest video (Sep 2025). blogTO is tier (c) here and the pages returned 403 on 27 Sep 2026; no CP24/CBC/Star confirmation found. Do not use the Parkdale seizure as a "closure" without a (b) source.
2. **"382 Popeyes restaurants in Canada as of July 15, 2026" / "Ontario 257"** — ScrapeHero location-scrape (c). Do not use; the only usable current figure is RBI's "380" (Jul 2025).
3. **"over 348 Canadian locations"** — Swoop Funding franchise-finance page (c).
4. **All menu prices from PriceListo, PriceMyMeal, popeyesmenu.ca, canadamenuhub, menupricetoday, dealdeal.ca, Medium "Camenu"** (e.g., "Chicken Sandwich C$8.49", "8-piece Family Meal $34.49", "value items from $3.98") — tier (c). Not to be cited.
5. **"Two proposed class actions in Ontario courts" / "2026 payouts"** — lawfold.com, billjoneslaw.com, mlellislaw.com (SEO law-marketing). No court record found. Do not use.
6. **Luffy Bento Bundle "$13.99"** — Yahoo/aggregator copy; the CNW release itself says $9.99*. Use $9.99.
7. **2026 ONSC 1823 details** (judge, exact list of added defendants, that a Popeyes Canada executive was added as an individual defendant) — seen only in a minicounsel.ca aggregator snippet; minicounsel returned 429 and CanLII blocked automated access on 27 Sep 2026. Pull the decision directly from CanLII before use.
8. **Verdict Food Service termination-notice quotes** ("significant discrepancies", "rodent activity") — Verdict has no byline and relies on Economic Times/court filings; the Globe (23 Oct 2025) independently reports the default and termination notices. Cite the Globe, not Verdict.
9. **Motley Fool transcript naming "Peter Prud'homme"** — transcription error; the RBI release and 10-K say Peter Perdue.
10. **Toronto Life "What the cluck" (Jun 2025)** — returned 403; not opened; not relied on.
11. **Popeyes presence in Quebec / any provincial exit** — no (a)/(b) source found either way (Yelp/TikTok only). Do not state Quebec facts.
12. **NRN's "down 4.9%" for Q4 2025** vs RBI release "−4.8%" — use RBI's figure.
13. **Wikipedia (Popeyes; Restaurant Brands International)** — pointer only for 1984 Canadian entry and 2017 acquisition; both facts are confirmed by the company release (a) and 10-K (a) and should be cited to those.
14. **Employee allegations of halal mislabelling and "unsanctioned meat"** (blogTO/Facebook video) — tier (c) and food-related; excluded under house rules.
15. **Sailormen Inc. bankruptcy (Jan 2026) and ~20 U.S. closures** — U.S. only (Georgia/Florida); do not present as Canadian.
16. **Popeyes Canada corporate registry entry** — not checked (registries not machine-readable in session). Legal entity name "Popeyes Louisiana Kitchen, Inc." rests on the 10-K trademark note and court reporting.

==================================================================

# Swiss Chalet and St-Hubert (Recipe Unlimited / Groupe St-Hubert) — "Paid the Price" dossier (chicken businesses only)

Prepared 27–28 September 2026 for a Canadian consumer-investigation YouTube channel. Every URL below was opened from the research environment on the date shown ("opened 28 Sep 2026" unless stated; the session ran overnight 27–28 Sep). Tier labels: (a) primary — regulator, court, securities filing, the company's own release/site, StatCan; (b) named outlet with byline and date; (c) aggregator/blog/SEO/review site/AI summary — flagged only, never a source; (d) documented consumer/media record with a named survey and methodology.

House rules applied: no health, nutrition or food-safety commentary (the one CFIA item is listed for the record only, without comment); every legal item is labelled allegation / finding / settlement / admission / company statement / pending; company claims are attributed ("the company says"); prices only from company channels or dated (b) reports; store counts only from filings, company releases/site or (b) reports, with exact wording; nothing "we tested"; anything not confirmed at (a)/(b)/(d) is in section 9.

**Access notes.** Web search was unavailable for this run; discovery was done through Google News RSS (links resolved to publisher URLs) and each publisher page was then opened directly. Blocked from this environment: SEDAR+ (JavaScript app), CanLII (HTTP 403), st-hubert.com (HTTP 403 on every path, including the order site), order.swisschalet.com (403), Quebecor sites (journaldemontreal.com, tvanouvelles.ca, journaldequebec.com, silo57.ca — 403), Castanet (403), HRImag (403), Kingston Whig-Standard and Kingstonist (blocked), Reuters, Toronto Star, Windsor Star via the fetch tool (but the Windsor Star page opened via curl), web.archive.org. Where a page could not be opened, the item is in section 9 as headline-only.

---

## 0 What the channel already said (February 2026) — do not repeat, go beyond

Video: "7 Canadian Restaurants That Got Too Greedy and Paid the Price", Canadian Counter, https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3daX4-GmSUM (52,566 views at 28 Sep 2026; transcript pulled 28 Sep 2026). Swiss Chalet was item #2. Verbatim transcript lines on Swiss Chalet:

- "British Columbia no longer has a single Swiss Chalet location. The last restaurant in the province, located in Burnaby, closed in August 2022 after multiple lease extensions. The general manager told local news the closure was due to economic reasons."
- "This followed a series of closures including the Kelowna location and the Victoria and Port Coquitlam restaurants."
- "According to Recipe Unlimited, supply chain disruptions, staffing challenges, and the impacts of the Ukraine-Russia conflict contributed to operational difficulties."
- "In 2024, the Oakville location remained closed for most of the year for upgrades. In 2025, the Windsor location permanently closed after nearly 30 years of operation."
- "According to reviews on Trustpilot and Tripadvisor, customers complain about increased prices, decreased quality, and poor service… One reviewer wrote that the festive special was a joke and an insult…"
- "Swiss Chalet is owned by Recipe Unlimited Corporation, which also owns Harvey's, The Keg, and several other restaurant brands." / "Recipe Unlimited, which took the company private in 2022 through a deal with Fairfax Financial…"

Corrections the new script must carry (all sourced below): (1) The Keg was separated from Recipe on 25 Sep 2025 (Fairfax 2025 Annual Report) — before the Feb 2026 video aired, so "also owns The Keg" was already wrong. (2) The Trustpilot/Tripadvisor passage is tier (c) and does not count as "paid the price". (3) "Victoria and Port Coquitlam" closures and "Oakville closed most of 2024 for upgrades" were not confirmed at (a)/(b) in this run (section 9). (4) The channel's June 2026 chicken-chains script says the "founding family [is] still holding a stake" — Fairfax's 2025 Annual Report says Recipe repurchased the Phelan family's shares in 2025, leaving Fairfax with 100%.

What is new in this dossier: St-Hubert's October 2024 price freeze and cuts after an admitted double-digit fall in dining-room traffic; the Napanee Swiss Chalet/Harvey's franchisee litigation (2023–2025, 2024 ONSC 2050); St-Hubert's 2025 decision to shrink its restaurants and its 2026 closures (Québec City Laurier, Montreal Village, Valleyfield, Rigaud); Swiss Chalet's 2026 Ontario closures (Oakville Cross Ave.; Mississauga Leanne Blvd. slated for demolition); the parent's system-wide count falling from 1,330 to "nearly 1,100"; and the Fairfax-filing record that contradicts a "collapse" narrative (record system sales 2023, +4% same-restaurant sales 2025).

---

### 3b Swiss Chalet — dated (b) reports

- Globe and Mail, 9 Aug 2022: "Like other restaurant owners, however, the company has been raising some menu prices, and continues to feel the effects of significant inflation in food prices and by a tight labour market." (No figure given.)
- Complex Canada, Louis Pavlakos, 15 Nov 2023: reports a "throwback" ad showing the Festive Special at $5.75 in 1990 versus $17.49 in 2023, with a Bank of Canada inflation-adjusted equivalent of $11.57. The underlying source is a tweet, so this is (b) reporting a (c) artefact — usable only if the 1990 ad and the 2023 price are independently confirmed (section 9). https://www.complex.com/life/a/louispavlakos/canada-reacts-price-of-swiss-chalet-festive-special (opened 28 Sep 2026).
- No documented shrinkflation report on Swiss Chalet at (a)/(b) was found.

### 3c St-Hubert — the documented value reversal (October 2024)

- Company release, Boisbriand, 9 Oct 2024 (a): "gel des prix" on all main dishes; price reductions on "une centaine" of menu items (starters, drinks, desserts) averaging about 11%; four new lunch plates "à partir de 12 $"; add-on combos "4 $" at lunch and "8 $" with the dinner table d'hôte. Richard Scofield: "L'inflation peut faire mal aux Québécois… nous voulons revoir notre équation de valeur." URL above (opened 28 Sep 2026).
- La Presse, Nathaëlle Morissette, 9 Oct 2024 (b): Scofield "admet néanmoins que les salles à manger de la chaîne ont enregistré une baisse de l'achalandage se situant « dans les deux chiffres »"; "l'aiguille des prix des plats principaux n'a pas bougé"; reductions "autour de l'assiette" — "baisse moyenne d'environ 11 %"; "quatre nouveaux plats à partir de 12 $"; "c'est possible pour une famille de quatre, un mardi soir avec une bouteille de vin, de manger à moins de 70 $ chez St-Hubert"; "chaque Québécois mangeait chez St-Hubert quatre à cinq fois par année… deux ou trois fois annuellement"; described by Scofield as "l'un des « plus gros virages dans le menu dans l'histoire de St-Hubert »"; on portion sizes, "la réduction des assiettes n'a pas fait partie des options envisagées" (company statement).
- 25 Sep 2026 release (a): on 29 Sep 2026 a quarter or half chicken "à prix festif" in all rotisseries (Quebec, Ontario, New Brunswick), price "to be announced Monday"; free dessert coupon with $30+ purchases since 8 Sep 2026. The 29 Sep price was not published at the time of writing (section 9).
- st-hubert.com menu prices could not be opened (403 on every path) — section 9.

## 4 Documented events 2022–2026, in date order

Format: date — what happened — who said it — tier — status.

Pre-window context (not "price paid" in the 2022–2026 window):
- 7 Feb 2020 — Kamloops Swiss Chalet/Harvey's (1395 Hillside Dr., open seven years, 27 staff) closed; "Management says Recipe Unlimited decided to close the Kamloops location for 'financial reasons'." CFJC Today, James Peters, 3 Feb 2020 (b), https://cfjctoday.com/2020/02/03/swiss-chalet-kamloops-location-to-close-permanently/ — company statement.
- Nov 2021 — West Kelowna closure (headline only; Castanet blocked — section 9).
- Dec 2021 — Burnaby closure announced for 27 Dec, then a "last-minute reprieve"; a 90-day lease agreement "will extend operations until March 31" 2022. Daily Hive, Hanna McLean, 3 Jan 2022 (b), https://dailyhive.com/vancouver/bc-swiss-chalet-not-closing — reported fact.

2022
- Early 2022 — Chatham, Ont. Swiss Chalet on Keil Drive South "closed earlier in 2022"; Recipe's franchising manager Kevin Winters said a new free-standing site was planned for 2023 and a franchisee was being sought. CK News Today, Allanah Wills, 2 Jul 2022 (b), https://cknewstoday.ca/chatham/news/2022/07/02/franchisee-wanted-new-revamped-swiss-chalet-chatham — company statement.
- 2 Aug 2022 — Recipe Q2 2022 results (as reported): "system sales were up 55 per cent to $873 million. Recipe Unlimited had 1,223 restaurants at the end of the quarter, down from 1,330 last year." CBC 9 Aug 2022 (b). CEO Frank Hennessey on the 3 Aug call: "Some of Recipe's brands have seen a decline in guest traffic, while at others, traffic is now above 2019 levels… So, it really is a bit of a mix." Globe 9 Aug 2022 (b). The Q2 memo, per Burnaby Now: "the food services industry continues to experience disruptions caused by the COVID-19 pandemic… supply chains and staffing levels, plus the Ukraine-Russia conflict"; "Multiple economic sectors have all opened at once, which has created a significant labour shortage in North America." Vancouver Is Awesome 19 Aug 2022 (b) — company statement / disclosure.
- 9 Aug 2022 — Fairfax proposal (section 1) — company statement.
- 21 Aug 2022 — Burnaby (3860 Lougheed Hwy) closed, "the last standing restaurant in the Swiss Chalet chain in all of B.C."; "The restaurant's general manager told the NOW the closure is due to economic reasons." VIA 19 Aug 2022 (b). Daily Hive 5 Aug 2022 (b), https://dailyhive.com/vancouver/swiss-chalet-burnaby-bc-closing, gives the lease-extension history (Dec 2021 reprieve; 90-day deal to 31 Mar 2022; May 2022 extension "through end of summer"). Daily Hive 23 Aug 2022 (b), https://dailyhive.com/vancouver/swiss-chalet-burnaby-bc-closed, quotes Swiss Chalet: "We continue to evaluate sites across the country for potential new Swiss Chalet locations." — documented exit from a province (already used by the channel).
- 1 Sep 2022 — definitive arrangement agreement (section 1).
- 28 Oct 2022 — privatization closed; delisting (Fairfax 2022 AR, a) — filing.
- 9 Dec 2022 — Mississauga Swiss Chalet at 6430 Erin Mills Pkwy closed; "eight Swiss Chalet locations remain in Mississauga". INsauga, Lucy Mazzucco (b), https://www.insauga.com/a-swiss-chalet-location-in-mississauga-has-closed/ — reported closure (reason not stated).
- FY2022 — Fairfax 2022 AR letter: "2022 represented a return to normalcy for Recipe. System sales increased to Cdn$3.4 billion, up 27% from 2021 and 2.4% higher than 2019!… management is focused on returning the company to pre-COVID level profitability, optimizing the brand portfolio to maximize cash flows" (a) — company statement.

2023
- 1 Jun 2023 — Swiss Chalet release: five openings in 2023; Mark Eaton (Chief Development Officer, Real Estate): "We are in growth mode and on the lookout for different locations in communities we believe Swiss Chalet can really thrive in." (a) — company statement.
- 10 Mar / 17 Mar 2023 — former Burnaby site taken by a dim-sum restaurant (Burnaby Beacon; Burnaby Now) — confirms no return to BC (b, listing only; pages not needed).
- 13 Dec 2023 — Napanee, Ont.: Recipe locked the franchisees (Morgan and Mishelle Rayer; company First of Five Incorporated) out of the combined Harvey's/Swiss Chalet; 26 employees. Global News, Shane Gibson, 22 Jan 2024 (b), https://globalnews.ca/news/10242434/franchisee-staff-locked-out-of-napanee-harveys-everyone-was-just-shocked/ — allegation (franchisee says he deducted HVAC repair costs from rent with Recipe's agreement: "We talked to them, we explained that we were doing that, they agreed."); Recipe did not respond to Global News.
- FY2023 — Fairfax 2023 AR: "Recipe… achieved record system sales in 2023. Sales increased to Cdn$3.7 billion, up 9% from 2022 and 5.6% higher than 2019… over Cdn$150 million in free cash flow and reduced overall leverage to less than 2.5x." (a) — company statement.

2024
- 7 Mar 2024 — Mississauga Swiss Chalet at 2975 Eglinton Ave. W. closed and relocated to 6975 Meadowvale Town Centre Circle (INsauga, Karen Longwell, b, https://www.insauga.com/closure-swiss-chalet-closes-and-reopens-in-new-location-in-mississauga/); a Chipotle later took the old site (INsauga 18 Feb 2025, b, https://www.insauga.com/heres-what-will-replace-an-old-swiss-chalet-in-mississauga/) — relocation, not a net closure.
- Apr 2024 — First of Five Incorporated v. Recipe Unlimited Corporation, 2024 ONSC 2050: the court found the motions judge's reasons inadequate, ordered a new hearing before a different judge and "stressed 'the importance of Recipe's undertaking not to terminate the franchise agreement pending the determination of the injunction motion'", citing fair-dealing obligations under the Arthur Wishart Act. Law Times, Angelica Dino, 24 Apr 2024 (b), https://www.lawtimesnews.com/news/general/ontario-superior-court-upholds-fair-dealing-in-franchise-dispute/385710 — court finding (interim). Napanee Beaver, Adam Prudhomme, 10 May 2024 (b), https://napaneebeaver.ca/napanees-harveys-swiss-chalet-wins-legal-right-to-re-open/: Justice William LeMay allowed the franchisees back "pending a full hearing"; restaurant reopened; "Of 26 employees locked out… 24 have returned"; Recipe's position, per the Beaver, was that the franchisees were "poorly managing the restaurant" (allegation); the main dispute "will proceed to trial in 2025".
- Jul 2024 — Berthierville, Que. St-Hubert closed after a flood; reopened 13 Oct 2025 after ~15 months and "environ 3 millions de dollars" (L'Action d'Autray, 2 Apr 2026, b, https://www.lactiondautray.com/article/actualites/reouverture-officielle-de-la-rotisserie-st-hubert-de-berthierville/) — not a market-driven closure.
- 13 Sep 2024 — Ontario Small Claims Court awarded a former Swiss Chalet worker $600 termination pay and ordered him to pay $8,000 in costs (HR Law Canada, b, paywalled summary, https://hrlawcanada.com/2024/09/former-swiss-chalet-worker-awarded-600-in-termination-pay-after-progressive-discipline-policy-ignored/) — finding; employer entity (franchisee vs Recipe) not visible; minor.
- 8–9 Oct 2024 — St-Hubert price freeze and ~11% cuts on ~100 items; Scofield admits double-digit dining-room traffic decline (section 3c) (a + b) — company statement / admission. La Presse headline: "Temps durs en restauration | St-Hubert revoit ses prix".
- FY2024 — Fairfax 2024 AR: "Recipe surpassed its record-breaking system sales in 2023 (adjusted for the 53rd week) with system sales of Cdn$3.6 billion in 2024. Revenue was up 0.5%… Cdn$114 million in free cash flow"; "Recipe had operating income of $120 million" (US$) (a), https://www.fairfax.ca/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Fairfax-Financial-2024-Annual-Report.pdf — company statement.

2025

## 7 Consumer and media record (tier d only)

- BrandSpark Most Trusted Awards 2026 (release 20 Nov 2025; BrandSpark International; "45,394 Canadian shoppers", "240,033" brand evaluations across "363 categories"): Swiss Chalet named most-trusted "Casual Restaurant for Family Dining" in the Atlantic, Ontario and West regions; St-Hubert in Quebec. https://www.newswire.ca/news-releases/2026-brandspark-most-trusted-r-awards-reveal-the-brands-canadians-trust-most-in-the-largest-survey-of-its-kind-in-canada-812637142.html (opened 28 Sep 2026). This cuts against a "lost trust" script line; use it for fairness.
- Leger Reputation 2026 (study "evaluates more than 330 Canadian companies' reputation each year across 29 different sectors"): the published top-10 image contains no restaurant; the full booklet was not downloadable, so no Swiss Chalet/St-Hubert placement can be stated. https://leger360.com/reputation/ and https://leger360.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/LEGER_Reputation_2026_gagnants_web_EN_CAN.png (opened 28 Sep 2026). Do not cite a Leger rank for either chain without the booklet.
- Company-disclosed traffic (not a survey, but the only documented traffic figure): St-Hubert dining-room traffic down "dans les deux chiffres" (Scofield, La Presse, 9 Oct 2024); frequency "quatre à cinq fois par année" historically vs "deux ou trois fois" now (same).
- Review sites (Trustpilot, Tripadvisor, Google reviews quoted by INsauga) — tier (c); not usable.

## 8 VERDICT for the script

**STRONG — but not for the reason the February video gave.** There is a documented, dated set of consequences in Canada for both chicken brands, several of them the company's own words, and they go beyond the BC exit and Windsor closure already aired. The parent's own filings, however, show record system sales in 2023 and +4% same-restaurant sales in 2025, so the honest framing is "the chicken chains retreated, shrank and cut prices while the owner reported record sales", not "collapse".

Strongest items (use these):
1. **St-Hubert's October 2024 value reversal, with an admitted double-digit traffic loss.** Company release 9 Oct 2024 (a): price freeze on all main dishes, ~11% average cuts on about 100 items, $12 lunch plates. La Presse the same day (b): the president admits dining-room traffic fell "dans les deux chiffres" and that Quebecers now eat there "deux ou trois fois" a year instead of "quatre à cinq". This meets the "public price rollback/value-menu change after a documented price increase" test (the Globe recorded Recipe "raising some menu prices" in Aug 2022), and the traffic admission is the company's own.
2. **The Napanee franchisee lockout and litigation (Dec 2023 → 2025), First of Five Inc. v. Recipe Unlimited Corp., 2024 ONSC 2050.** A Swiss Chalet/Harvey's franchisee locked out by the franchisor, 26 staff out before Christmas, a court ordering a fresh hearing and citing the franchisor's fair-dealing duty, the restaurant reopened pending trial. Label carefully: allegations on both sides; interim court findings; final outcome unknown/pending.
3. **A shrinking footprint by the companies' own wording, plus 2025–2026 closures.** Recipe: 1,330 restaurants (mid-2021) → 1,223 (mid-2022) → "nearly 1,100" (2026, partly the Keg spin-out). Swiss Chalet: "more than 200" (Jun 2023) → "180" on its own site (Mar 2025, per Windsor Star) → "over 190/195/almost 200" (Sep 2026); no BC, SK or QC on the franchising page. St-Hubert: 124 → 121 → "plus de 120"; new restaurants cut from 300 to 175 seats ("nos restaurants, on les rapetisse"); employees from 10,000 to ~7,200 (Radio-Canada). Dated closures: Windsor (May 2025), Oakville Cross Ave. (Jul 2026), Mississauga Leanne Blvd. slated (Sep 2026), Québec Laurier (Jan 2026, franchisee cites a 30% traffic loss), Montreal Village (autumn 2026), Rigaud Express (Mar 2025), Valleyfield (Mar–Apr 2026, franchisee out, corporate takeover).

Fairness lines the script should carry: BrandSpark 2026 named Swiss Chalet and St-Hubert the most-trusted family casual restaurants in their regions; Fairfax reports record system sales (2023) and +4% SRS (2025); St-Hubert announced a $50M plan with nine openings; Swiss Chalet opened five restaurants in 2023 and Recipe bought Olive Garden Canada. Do not say the chains are "collapsing" or "dying".

---

## 9 UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

1. Regular à-la-carte Swiss Chalet prices (Quarter Chicken Dinner, Half Chicken, Festive Special 2025) — not captured; swisschalet.com requires a location and order.swisschalet.com returned 403. Only the promotional prices in section 3a are usable.
2. Festive Special "$5.75 in 1990 vs $17.49 in 2023" — Complex Canada reporting a Twitter "throwback" ad; neither price verified from a company source. Do not use without the ad and a 2023 company price.
3. St-Hubert 29 Sep 2026 "prix festif" amount — announced as forthcoming; no release found by 28 Sep 2026.
4. St-Hubert menu prices from st-hubert.com — site blocked (403) from this environment; nothing captured.
5. Journal de Montréal/TVA Nouvelles, 3 Apr 2026, "St-Hubert ferme deux rôtisseries: près de 40 employés touchés" — headline only; pages blocked; which two restaurants and the company's statement unverified (Valleyfield inferred from Néomédia).
6. Journal de Montréal, 19–20 Jul 2026, "Après 50 ans, la rôtisserie St-Hubert du Village ferme…" — headline only (the Radio-Canada version is verified and says the site opened in 1977).
7. TVA Nouvelles, 4 Sep 2026, "« On s'est dit qu'il était temps de fermer », clame le propriétaire d'un St-Hubert en raison de quatre ans de travaux" — headline only; location not confirmed.
8. Journal de Québec, 6 Oct 2025, Laurier St-Hubert to move "plus de 40 ans après son ouverture" — headline only (Radio-Canada Jan 2026 version verified).
9. Castanet, 5 Nov 2021 ("Swiss Chalet West Kelowna will close its doors Nov. 7") and 9 May 2025 ("New Smitty's franchise to move into former Swiss Chalet on Westside") — headlines only; Castanet blocked. Kelowna closure is pre-window anyway.
10. The February 2026 video's "Victoria and Port Coquitlam" Swiss Chalet closures — not found at (a)/(b) in this run. A Nov 2020 Vancouver Is Awesome headline ("Two Canadian chain restaurants call it quits at neighbouring Metro Vancouver locations") was not opened; pre-window in any case.
11. The February 2026 video's "In 2024, the Oakville location remained closed for most of the year for upgrades" — not found at (a)/(b). The verified Oakville event is the July 2026 Cross Ave. closure for redevelopment.
12. All Trustpilot/Tripadvisor/Google-review material in the February video — tier (c); do not reuse.
13. Kingstonist, 23 Jan 2025 (interlocutory injunction granted) and 1 May 2024; Kingston Whig-Standard, 9 Feb and 2 May 2024 (right to appeal/reopen) — headlines only; pages blocked. Final outcome of the Napanee case unknown — say "pending" or "no public outcome found".
14. CanLII text of 2024 ONSC 2050 — not opened (403); rely on Law Times/Napanee Beaver summaries and label as such.
15. Recipe's own Q2 2022 results release and 2021 AIF/MD&A — not opened (SEDAR+ inaccessible). The 1,223/1,330 counts and "$873 million" system sales are as reported by CBC/Globe.
16. Reported Recipe bid for MTY Food Group (MarketScreener/Acquiremedia, 2 Feb 2026) — unconfirmed by any party; no deal found through Sep 2026. Do not use.
17. L'Express Franchise figures ("91 franchisés"; Recipe "plus de 1 200 restaurants") — trade outlet citing a company communiqué not opened directly; use only as "the trade press reports".
18. St-Hubert employee count: Radio-Canada's "10 000 → environ 7200" vs the company's "plus de 6 000" — inconsistent; if used, quote Radio-Canada with attribution and note the company figure.
19. HRImag, 25 Sep 2026 (St-Hubert 75 ans) and 11 Apr 2025 — blocked; not used.
20. 604 Now (Dec 2021, "Ontario and Alberta… dozens of locations"), To Do Canada (Thanksgiving "under $20", Oct 2024; holiday menu Dec 2023), Foodgressing, Petit Petit Gamin ("pain à volonté", Dec 2024), Narcity, mtlblog, Wikipedia — tier (c); flagged, not used.
21. The June 2026 chicken-chains script line "founding family still holding a stake" — outdated; Fairfax 2025 AR says 100% Fairfax after the 2025 buy-back. Correct on air.
22. Tecumseh (500 Manning Rd.) Swiss Chalet still open in 2026 — not checked.
23. Recipe's current legal name/registration ("Recipe Unlimited Corporation" vs "Recipe Restaurant Group International") — no Ontario/federal registry lookup possible from here; use "Recipe Unlimited Corporation, which now trades as Recipe Restaurant Group" with the release URLs.

# KFC Canada — "Paid the Price" dossier

Prepared 27–28 September 2026 for a Canadian consumer-investigation YouTube channel.
Every URL below was opened on the date shown ("opened 27 Sep 2026" / "opened 28 Sep 2026"). Tier labels: (a) primary; (b) named outlet with byline/date; (c) aggregator/blog/SEO — flagged only, never a source; (d) documented consumer/media record with named methodology.

House-rule reminders applied throughout: no health/nutrition/food-safety commentary; every legal item labelled allegation / finding / settlement / admission / company statement / pending; company claims attributed ("the company says"); prices only from company channels or dated (b) reports; store counts only from filings, releases or (b) reports with exact wording.

**Access note.** kfc.ca could not be opened from the research environment on 27–28 Sep 2026 (HTTP 503 via fetch tool; "empty reply from server"/HTTP2 stream error via curl, five URL attempts). Menu prices below therefore come from KFC Canada's own press releases (company channel, tier a) and Yum! Brands' SEC filings, not from the live kfc.ca menu. Re-open kfc.ca before recording.

FY2015 and FY2011 10-Ks likewise contain no Canadian unit count (FY2015 mentions "acquisitions in Canada" affecting Company sales). Yum's quarterly earnings releases give Canada's share of KFC Division system sales (2%) and system-sales growth, but not units (see §4/§6).

Value-menu moves in the 2020s (documented at tier a/b): Twosdays $2.99 (Apr 2023); $4.95 Fill Up, Ontario-only (Mar–May 2024); "year-round commitment to value" framing (Nov 2025); Everyday Value Menu + Good Cluckin' Deals platform (Jul 2026). **Not documented at tier a/b:** the Toonie Tuesday price ladder ($2 → $2.22 → ... → $3.99) and the 2025–2026 "$4.49"/"$2.99–$4.29" Tuesday Special figures — these appear only on SEO menu-price sites (tier c) and are listed in §9. A documented "price rollback after a documented increase" therefore cannot be asserted; what is documented is a $2.99 two-piece Tuesday offer explicitly framed by the company as an inflation-era value play.

---

## 5 Legal and regulatory record

**Courts (current, 2022–2026)**
- *Reddick c. Kentucky Fried Chicken Canada Company et al.*, Cour supérieure du Québec (Chambre des actions collectives), no 500-06-001436-258. Status: application for authorization filed Nov 2025; **pending**; allegation only. Registry documents (16330.pdf application; 16679.pdf) exist but could not be opened (HTTP 403). Basis used: La Presse, 13 Nov 2025 (b). A related but separate Quebec case (Salvatoré, hidden fees) was authorized 17 Dec 2025 per La Tribune — not KFC; noted only as context, not opened.
- Ontario/other provincial courts: no decision involving Kentucky Fried Chicken Canada Company or a Canadian KFC franchisee in 2020–2026 was found. Searches run: `"Kentucky Fried Chicken Canada Company" decision 2022..2026 court OR tribunal OR "labour relations board"`; `"Kentucky Fried Chicken Canada" 2022..2026 site:canlii.org`; `"KFC Canada" franchisee "Arthur Wishart" OR rescission OR termination Ontario 2020..2024`; `KFC Canada franchisee bankruptcy OR receivership 2023..2026`. Result: nothing found (CanLII domain search returned unrelated decisions).

**Courts (historic, for context)**
- *Kentucky Fried Chicken Canada v. Scott's Food Services Inc. et al.*, 1998 CanLII 4427 (ON CA), 2 Nov 1998: franchisor v. master franchisee (Scott's Food Services, Scott's Hospitality) over the master franchise licence agreement — share-transfer consent and remodelling obligations. Outcome: **finding** — appeal by Scott's allowed; court rejected KFC's interpretation that transfers required consent and found only 5% of outlets needed upgrading. Source: vLex case page (a — court decision), https://ca.vlex.com/vid/kfc-can-v-scott-680834341 (opened 27 Sep 2026); trial-level decisions (1997) 41 O.T.C. 241 and 44 O.T.C. 366 listed at vLex.
- *Priszm Income Fund et al.* — CCAA initial order 31 Mar 2011, Ontario Superior Court of Justice (Commercial List), court file CV-11-9159-00CL, OSB file 0000058-2011-ON; monitor FTI Consulting Canada Inc.; monitor discharged 14 Dec 2011; debtor head office 101 Exchange Avenue, Vaughan. Source: Office of the Superintendent of Bankruptcy CCAA record (a), https://ised-isde.canada.ca/site/office-superintendent-bankruptcy/en/ccaa-records-list/ccaa-records-priszm-income-fund-et-al (opened 27 Sep 2026). Globe and Mail 31 Mar 2011 (b): "Priszm owed Yum Brands approximately $2 million in unpaid royalty payments"; "same-store sales fell 6% in 2010"; Bitove: "It's not just Priszm, the whole KFC system in Canada has been struggling"; Yum's Canadian GM: "After two years, it became clear to us, and to them, that they were not going to be able to meet their obligations." Status: **franchisee insolvency, concluded 2011–2012** (outside the 2022–2026 window).
- Revenu Québec v. Priszm (2012): Le Soleil (Pierre Couture, 31 Oct 2012) reports Revenu Québec sued the former Quebec PFK franchisee for 26 alleged violations relating to mandatory sales-recording modules, with "over $52,000 in unpaid fines" — **allegation/regulatory action against the former franchisee, 2012**; outcome not confirmed; Olympus Food took over 14 Sep 2012. https://www.lesoleil.com/2012/10/31/lancien-franchise-des-pfk-au-quebec-dans-les-griffes-du-fisc-2245c898e986ce3e1a818cf320595a13/ (b, opened 27 Sep 2026).

**Competition Bureau** — nothing found. Searches run: `KFC Canada Competition Bureau`; domain-restricted search of competition-bureau.canada.ca and canada.ca for `KFC OR "Kentucky Fried Chicken"` (only a CIPO trademark record, "KFC REAL. DEAL. & Design," no. 1650481, surfaced).

**CFIA** — nothing found for KFC Canada 2022–2026. Search run: `CFIA recall KFC Canada 2022..2026 inspection.canada.ca` (no KFC-specific record returned). No commentary recorded, per house rules.

**Labour boards / employment standards** — nothing found for a Canadian KFC entity or franchisee 2023–2025. Search run: `KFC franchisee Ontario "Ministry of Labour" OR "Employment Standards" OR "labour board" fine OR order 2023..2025 Canada`. (Australian KFC rest-breaks class action results appeared; not Canada — excluded.)

**Franchisee association litigation** — none found in Canada (see §1). U.S. NCAC v. KFC (2009–2011, Delaware Chancery) explicitly excluded Canadian franchisees per Priszm's chairman (Globe and Mail).

## 7 Consumer and media record (tier d only)

Nothing usable at tier (d) for KFC Canada was confirmed:
- YouGov, "Best bites 2026: Canada QSR rankings" (8 Apr 2026), https://yougov.com/en-us/reports/54476-canada-qsr-rankings-report-2026 (opened 27 Sep 2026): page states insights are "based on YouGov BrandIndex and Profiles data" but shows no sample size, fieldwork dates or KFC placement without download. **Do not use** until the report is obtained and KFC's placement and methodology are read.
- Gustavson Brand Trust Index (University of Victoria) 2022–2025: no KFC-specific placement located in opened material.
- Léger Reputation: no KFC placement located.
- Technomic Top 200 Canadian Chain Restaurant Report (2024 and 2025 editions): press pages opened (https://www.technomic.com/press-top-200-canadian-chain-restaurant-report-2024/ ; https://www.einpresswire.com/article/812570244/...) contain no KFC line; the "KFC ranked 8th" claim surfaced only in a search summary — see §9.
- U.S. ACSI 2025 (KFC satisfaction drop 81→77) is a U.S. survey — not applicable to Canada; excluded.
- Documented public reaction (not a survey): CBC (May–Jul 2024) documents a petition and boycott calls, and support, over the Ontario halal switch (tier b, §4). Review-site complaints (BBB, Trustpilot, Reddit) do not count and were not collected.

---

## 8 VERDICT for the script

**WEAK.** KFC Canada has a documented, dated consequence set inside 2022–2026, but it is thin and none of it is a closure programme, franchisee insolvency, judgment or settlement.

Strongest items (all dated, all tier a/b):
1. **Company-disclosed sales decline, 2026.** Yum! Brands' own earnings releases show KFC Canada system sales growth ex-F/X turned negative: **−1% in Q1 2026** (8-K, 29 Apr 2026) and **−1% in Q2 2026, −1% year-to-date** (8-K, 30 Jul 2026), after **+7% in FY2025** and **+6% in FY2024**. Caveat for the script: this is system sales (all restaurants), not same-store sales or traffic; Yum does not publish Canadian same-store sales. Also Q2 2022 was −1%.
2. **Pending class-action application naming Kentucky Fried Chicken Canada Company first, Nov 2025.** *Reddick c. Kentucky Fried Chicken Canada Company et al.* (Quebec Superior Court, 500-06-001436-258), alleging drip-priced mandatory delivery fees on PFK's online ordering (example: sandwich shown at $11.29, final $17.04 with a $5 delivery fee). **Allegation; authorization pending; 49 chains plus Moneris named** — KFC is one defendant among many. Source: La Presse, 13 Nov 2025.
3. **Value-menu pivot explicitly framed around inflation, 2023–2026.** Twosdays $2.99 (Apr 2023, company release cites "a time of high inflation" — verify verbatim), $4.95 Fill Up (Ontario only, Mar–May 2024), "Year-Round Commitment to Value" (Nov 2025), Everyday Value Menu + "Good Cluckin' Deals" (Jul 2026). These are value moves, not a documented rollback of a documented price increase — the Toonie Tuesday price ladder exists only on tier-c sites.

What cuts against "paid the price": the company's own store-count boilerplate rose from "more than 600" (2020–Aug 2025) to "more than 650" (Nov 2025–Jul 2026); Yum's CFO listed Canada among markets with 10+ KFC openings in Q4 2025; Yum's CEO named Canada as a 2026 Kwench expansion market; and no Canadian franchisee bankruptcy, franchisee-association action, Competition Bureau matter, judgment or provincial exit was found for 2022–2026. The heavy consequences on record (Priszm CCAA 2011; roughly 15% of its 428 KFC outlets abandoned by mid-2012; Quebec City losing all its PFK outlets by Dec 2019) predate the window and should be presented as history, not as the 2022–2026 "price."

---

## 9 UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

Items that could not be confirmed at tier (a)/(b)/(d) on 27–28 Sep 2026. Do not voice these.

Store counts / footprint
- "685 KFC restaurants in Canada as of May 7, 2026"; "Ontario 268 (39%)" — ScrapeHero location report (c). Also GIS Utils "July 2026" count (c).
- Wikipedia "List of countries with KFC franchises" / "KFC" article counts — pointer only (c).
- "620 franchised units" — CFA listing is company-supplied but undated; use only with "undated company listing" caveat.
- Priszm 2003 IPO detail ("466 KFC restaurants in seven provinces"; "Canadian affiliate of Yum Brands Inc. owned 50 per cent of Priszm") — Globe and Mail article surfaced in search but not opened; open https://www.theglobeandmail.com/report-on-business/priszm-ipo-brings-in-150-million/article18435313/ before use.
- Soul Foods Group Canada "over 350 restaurants" / "nearly 400" — LinkedIn/HospitalityOnline/OpCapita (c); FMI "48 KFC's in New Brunswick, Nova Scotia, and Newfoundland" — BeTheBoss listing (c); FMI's own site did not load.
- Quebec City "had 14 restaurants" — La Presse 29 Dec 2019 (b) surfaced in search but the page returned HTTP 403; Le Quotidien version opened confirms the closure but not the "14" figure.

Prices
- Toonie Tuesday price ladder ($2.00 1996–2000; $2.22; $2.49; $2.79; $2.99; $3.49; $3.79; $3.99 2022–2023; $2.99 2023–) — kfcmenucanada.com and similar SEO sites (c).
- "Tuesday Special $4.49 in 2025"; "$2.99–$4.29 in 2026 depending on province"; "KFC Canada has mostly moved away from calling it Toonie Tuesday" — SEO sites (c).
- 2024 Twosdays "$2.99 ... through May 12" — Curiocity/RedFlagDeals (c). KFC Canada's own X posts (18 Feb 2022 "Bringing back toonie tuesday for #Twosday"; 13 Mar 2024 "2 pieces of dark meat chicken or 2 Tenders for only $2.99") are company channel (a) but could not be opened; open before use.
- All combo/bucket prices on menuspricescanada, canadian-menus, menupricesincanada, pricemymeal, kfcmenucanada, canadamenuhub, kfcmenupricescanada, pricelisto (c).
- Twosdays release sentence "At a time of high inflation, rising food and grocery costs, with chicken prices the highest they have been in decades..." — returned by the fetch tool as release text; confirm verbatim against the GlobeNewswire page before quoting.

Corporate / legal
- KFC Canada head office "191 Creditview Rd, Unit 100, Vaughan, ON L4L 9T1" — Dun & Bradstreet, TheOrg, directories (c). Not confirmed on kfc.ca (unreachable) or in a filing.
- "Canadian KFC Franchieses Association," Kitchener, Ontario — D&B directory entry only (c); no evidence of activity or litigation.
- Reddick class action: filing date "7 Nov 2025" and class-period start "since 7 Nov 2022" come from search snippets of the registry; the registry page and PDFs (16330.pdf, 16679.pdf) returned HTTP 403. Confirm at the Registre des actions collectives before stating dates; confirm current status (authorization hearing/judgment) — none found.
- Sabir Sami's prior roles "GM of Yum! Canada and GM of the KFC Canada and Turkey businesses" — from a Yum press release surfaced in search (not opened).
- "The halal rollout has not been completed nationwide as of 2026" — thehalalfood.ca / halalhitlist (c). No company statement located either way.
- Speculation that Ottawa/Thunder Bay were excluded because "processors could not meet volume requirements" — trade-outlet inference, not a company statement.

Earnings-call remarks that likely concern Taco Bell, not KFC — do not attribute to KFC Canada
- Q4 2025 call: "fourth-quarter double-digit same-store sales growth in Canada where we successfully launched crispy chicken" (Turner) — inconsistent with KFC Canada's +4% Q4 2025 system sales; almost certainly Taco Bell Canada.
- Q1 2026 call: "2-year stacked same-store sales growth of ... 18% in Canada" (Roy) and "Canada will probably be the next market that gets to 100 units" (Turner) — Taco Bell International context.
- Q2 2026 call: "Quench beverage platform ... expanding to the U.K., Australia, and Canada" — KFC; usable, but the Alphastreet transcript page returned 403; the Q1 2026 Motley Fool transcript (opened) carries the equivalent statement.

Surveys / rankings
- "KFC ranked 8th among Canada's largest chains in 2024 (Technomic Top 200)" — appeared only in a search summary; not present on the opened Technomic/EIN pages.
- YouGov Canada QSR 2026 — report not obtained; KFC placement unknown.
- ACSI 2025 KFC drop — U.S. survey; not Canada.
- BBB, Trustpilot, Reddit, TikTok content — excluded by rule.

U.S. closures (not Canada) — do not import
- "KFC closed 207 U.S. restaurants Jan 2025–Mar 2026" (TheStreet/Yahoo); "312 U.S. closures Jul 2025–Jul 2026" (Local Falcon); "about 25 KFC restaurants abruptly close" (Midwest franchisee, NRN/Restaurant Business). All U.S.; none concern Canada.

# Dossier: Church's Texas Chicken Canada AND Dixie Lee Fried Chicken

Prepared 27–28 September 2026 for a Canadian consumer-investigation script. Every URL below was opened in this session on **28 September 2026** (session clock; brief dated 27 September 2026) unless marked "NOT OPENED". Tier labels: (a) primary; (b) named outlet with byline/date; (c) aggregator/Wikipedia/blog/review site — flagged only, never a source; (d) documented consumer/media record.

Research limits to disclose to the producer: the web-search budget for the session ran out after ~200 queries; CanLII, CBC, Sudbury.com, My Bancroft Now, BusinessWire, Journal de Québec, D&B and Yahoo Finance returned 403/503 or were unreachable from this environment. Items that depend on those pages are in Section 9 with the URL so the producer can open them.

## A8 Verdict — Church's Texas Chicken Canada: **WEAK**

Documented, dated "price paid" 2022–2026 in Canada: only a stall against the company's own targets, and a marginal count difference (company said "over 83" in Mar 2024; company locator lists 81 on 28 Sep 2026). No tier (a)/(b) closure story, lawsuit, regulator matter, or province exit. Strongest usable items:
1. Company target "100 locations in Canada over just the next three years" (17 May 2021) and ~100 by end-2022 (22 Jun 2022) vs "over 83" (26 Mar 2024) vs 81 listed (28 Sep 2026) — all company sources.
2. Manitoba and the Maritimes named as "next" (Nov 2021, Mar 2022); no locations in either on the company locator 28 Sep 2026; Quebec never entered.
3. Global count wording fell from "more than 1,500 locations in 26 countries" (30 Jan 2023) to "more than 1,400 locations in 22 countries" (2 Apr 2025, 13 Apr 2026) — company releases; no Canadian breakdown.

None found. No named survey covering Dixie Lee. Review-site material excluded.

## B8 Verdict — Dixie Lee: **WEAK**

There is a documented long-run shrinkage of the Ontario-based franchisor (SEC: ~31 Canadian franchises, 30 May 2008 → company locator: 4 Canadian locations, 28 Sep 2026) and a documented 2023 supply failure (Radio-Canada), but no tier (a)/(b) 2022–2026 closure with a stated cause, no bankruptcy/receivership record, no franchisee litigation, and no price rollback. The Maritimes/Quebec franchisor, which appears to hold most of the surviving stores, publishes no counts. Strongest usable items:
1. SEC 8-K (Jun 2008) "approximately 31 Dixie Lee franchises in Canada" vs the same franchisor's locator listing 4 Canadian locations (28 Sep 2026); DLII stopped all SEC reporting after 26 Aug 2008 and never filed audited financials post-merger.
2. Radio-Canada, 26 Apr 2023: month-long signature-sauce shortage across NB and Gaspésie, attributed by franchisee Tony Murray to the Ontario supplier plant running out of potato starch.
3. Company homepage claims expansion in "60 Foreign Countries" while its own locator lists 5 stores in 2 countries (28 Sep 2026) — attributable as a company claim vs company page.

---

# COMBINED VERDICT (chain = "churchs-dixielee"): **WEAK**

Neither chain has a tier (a)/(b)/(d) dated 2022–2026 consequence of the kind the script defines (closures per filings/releases, disclosed same-store decline, franchisee suits, Competition Bureau matter, judgment/settlement, price rollback, franchisee bankruptcy, master-franchise loss, province exit). What is documented: Church's Canada growth stalled against company targets (100 → "over 83" → 81 listed) with promised Manitoba/Maritimes/Quebec entries not on its locator; Dixie Lee's Ontario franchisor shrank from ~31 (SEC, 2008) to 4 Canadian stores on its own site (2026) and suffered a documented 2023 supply failure in the Maritimes.

---

# Dossier: Nando's Canada, Chester's Chicken Canada, Lee's Famous Recipe Canada, and other fried-chicken chains ("nandos-others")

Prepared 27–28 September 2026 for a Canadian consumer-investigation script. Every URL below was opened in this session on **28 September 2026** (session clock; brief dated 27 September 2026) unless marked "NOT OPENED". Tier labels: (a) primary; (b) named outlet with byline/date; (c) aggregator/Wikipedia/blog/review/sponsored — flagged only, never a source; (d) documented consumer/media record.

**Research limits to disclose to the producer.** The session's web-search budget was exhausted before this dossier began, so discovery relied on Google News RSS feeds (resolved to publisher URLs), direct company sites, the BC corporate registry (OrgBook BC), the UK Companies House register, and the Competition Bureau site search. CanLII returned HTTP 403 on every attempt; nandos.ca and nandos.ca/menu returned a "Vercel Security Checkpoint" (HTTP 429) on every attempt; jollibee.ca returned 503 / connection reset; the Wayback Machine returned 429; Corporations Canada's federal search requires JavaScript and returned no results in this environment; QSR Magazine, Business Wire, BIV, Straits Times (via WebFetch), Toronto.com, Mississauga.com, Inquirer.net and CBC returned 403/blocked. Items that depend on those pages are in Section 9 with the URL so the producer can open them.

---

## A8 VERDICT — Nando's Canada: **WEAK**

Documented, dated items inside 2022–2026:
1. Net store-count decline from **32** (company release, 10 Jan 2023) to **29** (Retail Insider and Noms, Mar–Apr 2026) — a company-attributed count in both cases.
2. Context only (outside window): 21 corporate-owned closures announced 15 May 2020, from 48 to 27, with the company's own "not commercially viable" wording; the chain has not regained its 2019 size.
No same-store-sales disclosure, franchisee action, class action, Competition Bureau matter, price rollback or bankruptcy was found. The 2022–2026 decline is small and partly explained by a fire (Kerrisdale, Oct 2023–Apr 2026) rather than a business decision, so the script should not present it as a collapse.

Company target: "planning to open 100 locations in the country in the next five years"; Minana: "100 stores would be something that's very realistic for us to do". | (b) Retail Insider, 19 Jun 2018 (above) |

Reading for the script: in June 2018 the company said 100 Canadian stores in five years (i.e., by 2023); its own release in August 2026 counts 28. That is a company-target shortfall, documented from the company's own statements, but it is **not** a closure, decline or legal consequence.

## B8 VERDICT — Jollibee Canada: **NONE**

Canadian network grew from 3 (2018) to 28 (2026) and the company has announced 26 more franchised units. The only documented shortfall is against the company's own 2018 "100 stores in five years" target — a missed projection, not a listed "price paid" consequence. The parent's global 2026 target cuts and 207 first-half closures are not attributed to Canada in any source opened.

## 8 OVERALL VERDICT for the script (chain group "nandos-others"): **WEAK**

Of the chains in this group, only Nando's Canada and Jollibee Canada are national with 20+ units and verifiable counts. Jollibee is growing (NONE). Nando's has a small, company-attributed net decline inside the window. Strongest usable items, in order:
1. **Nando's Canada: 32 restaurants (company release, 10 Jan 2023) → 29 (Retail Insider, 2 Apr 2026; Noms, 31 Mar 2026).** Wording: "the company said 32 in January 2023; by spring 2026 outlets citing the company put it at 29."
2. **Nando's Canada, context: 21 corporate-owned closures announced 15 May 2020 (48 → 27), the company saying the stores "have not been commercially viable for some time."** Outside the 2022–2026 window — use only as backstory.
3. **Jollibee Canada: 2018 target of "100 locations in the country in the next five years" vs. "28 company-operated restaurants in Canada" in August 2026.** A missed company projection, not a "price paid" item; if used, say exactly that.
Lee's Canada's 4 → 3 locator decline is real but the chain has under 20 units; Chester's Canadian footprint cannot be counted.

---

## 9 UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

1. **Chester's Chicken Canadian unit count** — no company or (b) figure exists; the company's own locator shows zero Canadian pages. Do not state any Canadian number.
2. **Chester's "over 1,200"/"over 1,300" locations** — two different company figures (release Sep 2026 vs. site Sep 2026); quote whichever you cite with its date; do not reconcile.
3. **Chester's Barrière, BC opening (Oct 2021)** — appears only via Mashed (c) citing North Thompson Star; original NOT OPENED.
4. **Nando's Canada current legal entity** — BC registry shows both "Nando's Chickenland" records as historical (2016, 2022); the operating entity today is unconfirmed. Do not name an entity as the operator without a registry check (Corporations Canada / Ontario).
5. **Nando's Canada "48 restaurants before 2020"** — derived (21 + 27), not a company-stated number.
6. **Nando's Canada menu prices** — only source is Narcity "Drive-Thru" paid content (19 Jan 2024) carrying company-supplied prices; nandos.ca not reachable. Use only as "Nando's advertised…" with the date, or not at all.
7. **Nando's Mississauga opening, 29 Mar 2024** — Mississauga.com NOT OPENED (403); headline only.
8. **Nando's Canada 2021 franchisee-support story** — Restobiz, 15 Oct 2021, returned a blank/challenge page: https://www.restobiz.ca/nandos-canada-putting-staff-and-franchisees-first/
9. **blogTO, 22 Apr 2020 "Popular Toronto chicken restaurant shut down by landlord due to non-payment of rent"** — surfaced in a Nando's news query; restaurant identity NOT confirmed (page 403). Do not attribute to Nando's.
10. **Nando's global count / Statista "Number of Nando's restaurants worldwide"** — (c), not opened.
11. **Jollibee Canada operating entity** — "JBM LLC" is Retail Insider's wording for the North American franchisor; the Canadian licensee/operating company is unconfirmed by registry.
12. **Jollibee "closes 207 restaurants" (TheStreet, 14 Aug 2026)** — page 403; the 207 figure is confirmed only from the company's Q2 release and is global, not Canadian.
13. **Jollibee Canada menu prices** — jollibee.ca unreachable; Toronto.com 7 Aug 2025 NOT OPENED.
14. **Jollibee "1851 Franchise" 2025 expansion article** — (c) franchise-marketing site; not used.
15. **Lee's Esquimalt closure** — inferred from absence on the company locator (4th store announced Dec 2022; 3 listed Sep 2026; /locations/esquimalt returns 404). No outlet report found. Say "no longer listed on the company's locator", not "closed", unless confirmed.
16. **Lee's total-location enumeration on leesfamousrecipe.com** — two automated reads gave 165 and 147 entries; unreliable. Use the company's "more than 130" wording instead.
17. **QSR Magazine, 2 Dec 2022 (Lee's fourth Canadian store)** and **Business Wire, 28 Jan 2025 / 10 Feb 2026 (Lee's)** — 403; content taken from the QSR Web and 24-7 Press Release carriers of the same releases.
18. **Chicken Delight (Winnipeg) unit count** — company site gives none; locations page blocked (Cloudflare). Prices seen on chickendelight.com 28 Sep 2026 (Family Celebration Dinner $56.95; Value Meal $11.99; Delight Duo $31.95; Super 7 $41.95) are company-channel prices but the chain is out of scope (count unknown).
19. **Chicken Chef 44 locations** — researcher enumeration of the company locator, not a company-stated total; some entries flagged by the company as temporarily closed.
20. **"Country Style Chicken"** — no such national chain found; likely confusion with Country Style (MTY coffee/donuts).
21. **CanLII, CFIA, labour-board searches** — not run (CanLII 403; others not attempted). "Nothing found" in Section 5 means not searched.
22. **Wikipedia / Mashed / franchising.com / 1851 / QSR sponsored content** — (c) pointers only; nothing from them is cited as fact.

# Mary Brown's Chicken — Fact-check dossier ("paid the price" claim, Canada, 2022–2026)

Prepared 27 September 2026 for a Canadian consumer-investigation YouTube channel.
Chain key: `marybrowns`. All URLs below were opened on 27 Sep 2026 unless marked **NOT OPENED** (server refused: 403/402); anything from a NOT-OPENED page is quarantined in Section 9.

Tier key: (a) primary — company release/site/registry/filing/regulator; (b) named outlet with byline+date; (c) aggregator/wiki/forum/menu-price site — flagged only, never cited; (d) documented consumer/media record with named methodology.

House-rule reminders applied: no health/nutrition/food-safety content; no uncharged wrongdoing stated or implied; corporate claims attributed ("the company says"); prices only from company channels or dated (b) reports; store counts only from company releases/site, franchise-body listing or (b) reports with exact wording.

Reading of the record (analyst note, inference from the rows above): every dated company figure from 2021 to 2026 is higher than the one before it (188 → ~215 → ~220 → 255 → 257 → 260+ → 280+ → 290+ → 300+). No source at any tier reports a net decline or a province exit. Two cautions: (i) the "over N" boilerplate is rounded and the net gain Feb 2025→Jun 2026 (~20) is smaller than the 26–33 gross openings per year reported for 2022–2023, so growth appears to have slowed and some closures/relocations are implied but not disclosed; (ii) minor discrepancy in 2021 openings — F&H says 21, Franchise Times says 19; the 2021 Retail Insider piece says 10 opened + 22 in process by June.

## 7 Consumer and media record (tier d only)

- **Canadian Franchise Association — Franchisees' Choice Designation.** A named, annual, franchisee-survey-based designation administered by the CFA. Company states it has received it for 13 consecutive years (Feb 2024–Feb 2025 boilerplate), 15 (Oct 2025–Jun 2026 boilerplate) and "16 Years" (Our Story statistics graphic). The CFA's own listing page for the brand was opened (https://cfa.ca/lookforafranchise/listing/mary-browns-chicken/, 27 Sep 2026); the CFA's designation-methodology page (https://cfa.ca/franchisecanada/franchisees-choice-designation/) surfaced in search but was not opened — treat the consecutive-year count as a company claim until that page is checked. This is a franchisee-satisfaction record, not a consumer record.
- **Deloitte Canada's Best Managed Companies — Platinum (2019–2025).** Named program; company claim only (not verified on Deloitte's site in this session).
- **Brand-trust / reputation rankings (Léger, Gustavson Brand Trust Index, "Canada's Most Trusted").** None found mentioning Mary Brown's for 2024–2026 (search run 27 Sep 2026). Nothing to cite.
- **"Canada's Best Chicken Sandwich" (Daily Hive).** Editorial ranking by Daily Hive's Dished section — no stated methodology → does **not** qualify as tier (d). Do not present as a survey.
- Review-site complaints (Tripadvisor/Yelp/Reddit) — excluded by house rules; none counted.

---

## 8 VERDICT for the script

**NONE.** On the documented record, Mary Brown's Chicken has no dated "price paid" in Canada for 2022–2026 under the script's own definition.

What the record shows instead:
1. **Continuous Canadian store-count growth at every dated company or (b) data point**: 188 (Jun 2021, Retail Insider) → "around 215" (Jun 2022, company) → "255" (Feb 2024, company) → "257" (Apr 2024, Franchise Times) → "over 280" (Feb 2025, company) → "over 290" (Oct 2025, company) → "over 300", 300th store in New Brunswick (Jun 2026, company). No filing, release or (b) report shows a net decline, a province exit, or a Canadian closure programme.
2. **Company-disclosed same-store sales are up, not down**: "17 consecutive years of same-store sales growth" (F&H, Dec 2022), +4.3% in 2023 on $310M system sales (Franchise Times, Apr 2024), "19 consecutive years" (company boilerplate, Oct 2025–Jun 2026). These are the company's own figures (privately held; no SEDAR+/SEC filings exist).
3. **No litigation, regulator, franchisee-association or insolvency item found** after 12 targeted searches (§5).

Closest items — none qualifies, listed so the script does not overreach:
- Jan 2022 Big Mary Monday promotional price raised to $4.99 "with rising food costs" (company statement, verification caveat in §3). This is a price increase, not a consequence; no documented rollback followed at tier (a)/(b).
- 28 Feb 2023 downtown St. John's Water Street unit closed 11 Mar 2023 and converted to the owner's other brand, Fat Bastard Burrito (VOCM). A single owner-initiated brand swap, pre-dating the 2024–2026 window of interest and not a decline.
- Nov 2025 closure of the Preston, England "MB Chicken" unit after roughly six months (Blog Preston — page not opened). Outside Canada; irrelevant to a Canadian "price paid" and unverified here.

Plain statement for the writers: if the segment needs a chain that "paid the price," Mary Brown's is not it on the evidence available; the honest framing is "a Canadian chicken chain that raised a promo price in 2022 and kept growing — the company says 19 straight years of same-store sales growth and 300+ stores." Any stronger claim would need a franchisee action, court record or company-disclosed decline that this research did not find.

---

## 9 UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

1. **"MB Chicken Inc." as the Canadian franchisor's legal name** (given in the assignment). Not found in any opened source; opened sources name "Mary Brown's Inc.", "MB International Brands" and "MBI Brands Inc." Confirm via Ontario Business Registry / Corporations Canada before use.
2. **"PI Enterprises Group" / "Roberts Family Office"** as Roberts' holding entity — appears only in Wikipedia (c pointer) and a LinkedIn title (c). The Globe and Mail (2011) names "Pilley's Island Enterprises". Do not name the holding company on air without a registry check.
3. **Big Mary Monday prior price of $3.99 before 4 Jan 2022** — RedFlagDeals forum thread only (c); thread could not be opened (402 paywall redirect). https://forums.redflagdeals.com/mary-browns-chicken-big-mary-monday-increases-4-99-starting-jan-4-2022-2510077/
4. **Big Mary Monday at $3.99 in Aug 2026** (would imply a rollback) — deal aggregator mealdeals.app (c). Not usable.
5. **Company Facebook post body text and date for the Jan 2022 increase** — page blocked when opened; only the URL slug and search snippet confirm the wording. Screenshot the live post before broadcast.
6. **Business Wire, 2 Nov 2021, "Mary Brown's Chicken Opens 200th Store in Canada"** — 403; search snippet claims "opened 20 stores in 2020 and opened 30 more in 2021" (conflicts with F&H's 18/21 and Franchise Times' 19 for 2021). Exact wording unverified. https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20211102006311/en/Mary-Browns-Chicken-Opens-200th-Store-in-Canada
7. **Newmarket Today, 200th-store story (Nov 2021)** — 403, not opened. https://www.newmarkettoday.ca/local-news/chicken-has-become-a-comfort-food-mary-browns-marks-200th-restaurant-opening-in-newmarket-4715085
8. **CBC, Feb 2024 ("Mary Brown's ... global", 1.7095765) and Mar 2025 ("leans into its Canadian roots in U.S. tariff fight", 1.7476186)** — both 403 (main and lite URLs). Likely usable (b) sources on international expansion and tariff/sourcing comments; re-open from a normal browser. https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/newfoundland-labrador/mary-browns-dylan-powell-tariffs-1.7476186 ; https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/newfoundland-labrador/mary-browns-global-1.7095765
9. **Restaurant Business Online (Fat Bastard acquisition, 2022) and QSR Magazine** — 403, not opened; the company release and CFA repost cover the same facts.
10. **Blog Preston, Nov 2025, "MB Chicken suddenly closes just six months after opening"** — 403, not opened; UK only; company reportedly did not respond to the outlet. https://www.blogpreston.co.uk/2025/11/mb-chicken-suddenly-closes-just-six-months-after-opening-with-fanfare/
11. **"Plans to open upwards of 50 new locations in 2025"** — appeared in search snippets (Daily Hive / Elite Franchise) but not in the 27 Feb 2025 release or Retail Insider text as opened. Do not attribute without locating the exact source line.
12. **Wikipedia figures**: "approximately 300 stores", "about 249 franchised", "21 corporate stores", CEO succession dates (Chahin 2018–2024, Samuelson CEO 2024) — pointer only; the Samuelson title in the company's own Feb 2025 release is "President and Global Chief Operating Officer", not CEO.
13. **Third-party location counts** (ScrapeHero "251 locations as of 10 Sep 2026", "247 as of 11 Mar 2026"; malls.fandom wiki) — scraped datasets (c); they undercount versus company releases and must not be used to imply closures.
14. **Menu-price aggregator prices** (canadianmenuwithprices, menuspricescanada, canadamenuhub, caeats, pricelisto, marrybrownmenupricescanada, DoorDash/Uber Eats listings) — tier (c); e.g., "Big Mary $8.89", "Big Mary combo $12.99", "9 pc $30.99". Not usable.
15. **CFA Franchisees' Choice methodology page** and **Deloitte Best Managed** listing — not opened; consecutive-year counts remain company claims.
16. **Franchise start-up cost figures** — Franchise Times (Apr 2024) says $840,000; CFA listing says "$875K+"; no franchise disclosure document was obtainable (FDDs are not public in Canada). Use only with attribution to the specific source.
17. **Trade Commissioner Service page** wording "hundreds of locations across Canada" — vague; use company releases for counts.
18. **Fat Bastard Burrito unit count 2025–2026** — only derivable by subtraction (MBI "over 400" minus Mary Brown's "over 300"); not stated anywhere. Do not state a number.

# Fact-check dossier: Chick-fil-A Canada and Wingstop Canada

Prepared 27 September 2026 for the "paid the price" script. All URLs below were opened 27–28 September 2026 (UTC; session clock crossed midnight) unless marked "NOT OPENED". Source tier in brackets: (a) primary, (b) named outlet with byline/date, (c) aggregator/flag-only, (d) documented consumer/media record.

Method note: the shared web-search budget for this session was exhausted part-way through; remaining work used direct fetches of company, regulator and outlet URLs, the companies' own sitemaps, and Wingstop Canada's own online-ordering API (api.wingstop.ca), which is the back end of wingstop.ca. Where a search could not be run, it is listed as such in section 5.

Bottom line for both chains: **VERDICT NONE.** Both chains are in documented net expansion in Canada across 2022–2026, with no filing, release, regulator record or (b) report found showing a closure, net store-count decline, disclosed Canadian same-store-sales or traffic decline, franchisee action, class action, Competition Bureau matter, court judgment, price rollback, franchisee bankruptcy, loss of master franchise or provincial exit.

---

# PART A — CHICK-FIL-A CANADA

## A1 Ownership and Canadian operator

| Fact | Source | Tier |
|---|---|---|
| Parent: Chick-fil-A, Inc., privately held, Atlanta, Georgia (5200 Buffington Road). | Chick-fil-A press releases (footers) e.g. https://www.chick-fil-a.com/press-room/chick-fil-a-announces-expansion-in-canada (opened 27 Sept 2026) | (a) |

No closure of a Canadian Chick-fil-A restaurant was found in any company release or (b) report (the 2014–2019 Calgary airport counter pre-dates the franchised chain and the window; see A9).

## A3 Price record

- Company channel: the Canadian menu page shows **no prices**; it states "Price and availability may vary by location." (https://www.chick-fil-a.ca/menu, opened 27 Sept 2026) (a). Prices are exposed only in the ordering app/in-store, which could not be captured here.
- No dated (b) report of Canadian Chick-fil-A menu prices, a price increase, shrinkflation or a value-menu change was found (searches: CBC, Globe and Mail, Financial Post, Toronto Star, blogTO — see section 5 search log; the last search in that set could not be run because the search budget was exhausted).
- Documented menu changes (no prices stated): Spicy Chick-n-Strips and bottled Chick-fil-A Sauce, 21 Mar 2022 (https://www.chick-fil-a.ca/press-room/chick-fil-a-introduces-spicy-chick-n-strips-and-bottled-chick-fil-a-sauce-at-canadian-restaurants) (a); seasonal fall items 8 Sept 2022 (https://www.chick-fil-a.ca/press-room/chick-fil-a-canada-spices-up-fall-with-new-menu-items) (a); Mac & Cheese from 17 Mar 2025, "the second permanent menu addition since the brand opened its doors in Canada in 2019" (https://www.chick-fil-a.ca/press-room/say-cheese-canada-chick-fil-a-mac-and-cheese-is-here, dated 13 Mar 2025) (a).
- Menu-price aggregator figures (PriceMyMeal, PriceListo, menupricesincanada.com etc., including "Deluxe Meal +3.47%") are **(c) — flagged, do not use**. See A9.
- **Result: no usable Canadian price record; no documented increase or rollback.**

## A7 Consumer and media record (tier d only)

Nothing found. No named survey with year, publisher and methodology covering Chick-fil-A in Canada was located. (The NRN-reported 2022 Canadian average unit volume of "over $4 million" is a company figure relayed by a (b) outlet, not a (d) record.)

## A8 VERDICT (Chick-fil-A Canada): **NONE**

No documented, dated "price paid" in Canada 2022–2026. The record is the opposite: store count rose from 6 (Oct 2022) to 20 (end 2024) to about 30 open on the company's own site (Sept 2026), with announced entry into Alberta (2024) and British Columbia (2027–28). Strongest items are all expansion facts:
1. 2 Jan 2025 release: 20-store goal "met ... ahead of schedule" (a).
2. Company site, 27 Sept 2026: 31 listed locations, 30 with operating hours (a).
3. CFIA and Competition Bureau searches: no results (a).

## A9 UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE (Chick-fil-A)

1. Chick-fil-A Canada ULC incorporation date (19 Sept 2017, BC) and "began operations May 2019" — from a search excerpt of the US FDD; the PDF (franchisetimes.com) returned 403 and OpenCorporates (https://opencorporates.com/companies/ca_bc/BC1134490) returned 403. Confirm at BC Registry before use.
2. Any Canadian menu price for Chick-fil-A (e.g., aggregator "Deluxe Meal +3.47% ($0.60)") — (c) only. Do not use.
3. "31 Chick-fil-A locations in Canada as of September 15, 2026 / 23 in Ontario" — ScrapeHero (c). Our own company-sitemap count happens to match but use the company listing, not ScrapeHero.
4. "Calgary International Airport counter opened 2014, since shuttered" — search-summary claim (c), pre-window, unopened; do not use.
5. CBC News report on the 6 Sept 2019 Toronto opening (https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/toronto/chik-fil-a-toronto-opening-1.5273381) — returned 429/403; cite Daily Hive/Global News instead.
6. Court, labour-board and CanLII records — not searchable this session (403 / search budget). Status: UNSEARCHED.
7. Wikipedia "Chick-fil-A" — pointer only.
8. Franchise Times-hosted FDD PDFs and Scribd "Chick-fil-A FDD 2025" — not opened / (c) host.

CF Chinook Centre flagship (live DJ booth); JPK now master franchisee for Canada, Australia and New Zealand; ">200 locations across Canada and Australia over the next decade". | JPK Capital release (newswire.ca); Foodservice and Hospitality; Daily Hive Calgary | (a)/(b) | Company statement |

Observation for the script (state carefully, not as a consequence): the October 2025 plan was three Calgary openings in H1 2026 and 19 Ontario stores by end-2025. As of 28 Sept 2026 the company's own online-ordering system shows 20 Ontario stores and no Calgary store. Whether Calgary has opened could not be confirmed either way (the ordering list may lag, and web search was unavailable); this is **not** a closure, decline or other "price paid" item under the definition. Do not say Calgary was cancelled or delayed without a company statement or (b) report.

## B8 VERDICT (Wingstop Canada): **NONE**

No documented, dated "price paid" in Canada 2022–2026. The chain went from zero to 20 Ontario restaurants listed on its own ordering system between June 2022 and September 2026, with a 100-unit development commitment and a 2025 expansion of JPK's master rights to Australia/New Zealand. Strongest items (all expansion facts):
1. JPK Capital release, 6 Oct 2025: "opened 15 locations in Ontario"; Calgary flagship announced (a).
2. Company ordering API, 28 Sept 2026: 20 available Ontario restaurants, none closed/unavailable (a).
3. CFIA and Competition Bureau searches: no results; SEC 10-K: no Canadian legal or development issue (a).

# Dossier: "Worth-it" chicken alternatives — supermarket rotisserie/hot-counter programs (Costco, Sobeys/Empire, Loblaw) and chicken lines of non-chicken chains (Pizza Pizza "Chicken Chicken", A&W, McDonald's, Wendy's)

Chain label for the script: **alternatives**
Research date: 27 September 2026 (session clock read 28 Sept 2026 UTC; every URL below was opened this session, "opened 27 Sept 2026" unless noted).
Verdict requested for alternatives: **NONE** (verdicts not needed; this file exists to supply checkable prices, ownership and consumer-price commitments).

## Method note (read first)

- This session's web-search budget was exhausted before this task began. Every fact below came from **direct opens of known primary URLs** (SEC EDGAR 10-Ks, company press releases on Cision/newswire.ca, company investor sites, Empire's Voilà storefront, StatCan's Web Data Service API). No search engine was used; nothing here comes from aggregators.
- Several company retail sites (loblaws.ca, realcanadiansuperstore.ca, sobeys.com, mcdonalds.com/ca, web.aw.ca menu, pizzapizza.ca menu) returned HTTP 403 or JavaScript-only shells to this session. Prices from those channels are therefore **not** in this file and are listed in Section 9 for the team to check in-app.
- Tier labels: (a) primary; (b) named outlet with byline/date; (c) never cited; (d) documented consumer/media record.
- Price disclaimer for the script: Voilà prices are online-storefront prices as displayed to an un-located visitor; in-store hot-counter prices can differ by banner, store and region.

---

## 1 Ownership and Canadian operator

### 1.1 Costco (rotisserie chicken program)
- Canadian legal entity: **"Costco Wholesale Canada Ltd."** — copyright line on https://www.costco.ca/about-us.html (a; opened 27 Sept 2026). Same page (company claim, undated): Costco "serves more than 10 million members across Canada, and is one of the nation's largest retailers, averaging CDN$25 billion in sales per year."
- Parent: Costco Wholesale Corporation (Nasdaq: COST), Issaquah, Wash. — Q4 FY2026 release dateline, https://investor.costco.com/news/news-details/2026/Costco-Wholesale-Corporation-Reports-Fourth-Quarter-and-Fiscal-Year-2026-Operating-Results/default.aspx (a; opened 27 Sept 2026).
- Canada is a reported segment in the 10-K (employees: "Canada 55,000" at FY2025 vs 53,000 FY2024, 51,000 FY2023) — Costco 10-K filed 8 Oct 2025, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/909832/000090983225000101/cost-20250831.htm (a; opened 27 Sept 2026).

### 1.2 Sobeys / Empire (hot-counter and Voilà)
- **Empire Company Limited (TSX: EMP.A)**, headquartered Stellarton, Nova Scotia; food retailing "through wholly-owned subsidiary Sobeys Inc." Boilerplate verbatim: "With approximately $32 billion in annual sales and $17 billion in assets, Empire and its subsidiaries, franchisees and affiliates employ approximately 130,000 people." — Q1 FY2027 results release, 10 Sept 2026, https://www.newswire.ca/news-releases/empire-reports-eps-of-1-04-in-the-first-quarter-of-fiscal-2027-results-836389391.html (a; opened 27 Sept 2026).
- Banners listed in Empire's own site footer (a; https://www.empireco.ca/en/ opened 27 Sept 2026): Sobeys, Safeway, Foodland, IGA West, IGA extra, Les Marchés Tradition, Thrifty Foods, FreshCo, CHALO! FreshCo, Lawtons Drugs, Bonichoix, Needs, Voilà.
- Longo's: Empire's 10 Sept 2026 release refers to "nine Morelli's pharmacies currently operating within Longo's stores in the Toronto and Hamilton areas" with a plan "to convert these nine sites to Longo's Pharmacy locations" (a). Longo's products are sold on Empire's Voilà storefront (see Section 3). Longo's ownership percentage: not verified this session (Section 9).
- Voilà is named in the release as one of "the Company's e-commerce platforms Voilà (including curbside pickup), IGA.net, ThriftyFoods.com" (a).

### 1.3 Loblaw (PC/hot-counter)
- **Loblaw Companies Limited**; controlling parent George Weston Limited — https://www.loblaw.ca/en/who-we-are (a; opened 27 Sept 2026). Banners named on that page: Loblaws, Real Canadian Superstore, No Frills, Shoppers Drug Mart, Pharmaprix, T&T Supermarket, Provigo; Maxi named in Q3-2025 release.
- Boilerplate verbatim (a): "With more than 2,800 locations, Loblaw, its franchisees and Associate-owners employ more than 220,000 full- and part-time employees, making it one of Canada's largest private sector employers." — Q3 2025 release, 12 Nov 2025, https://www.newswire.ca/news-releases/loblaw-reports-revenue-growth-of-4-6-in-the-third-quarter-855695152.html; repeated in the 1 Sept 2026 release (Section 4).
- Note: the undated who-we-are page says "More than 2,400 Stores across Canada" while releases say "more than 2,800 locations" (locations includes pharmacy). Use the dated release wording.

### 1.4 Pizza Pizza / "Chicken Chicken"
- **Pizza Pizza Royalty Corp. (TSX-listed)** indirectly owns the Pizza Pizza and Pizza 73 rights and marks and receives royalties on a Royalty Pool of franchised restaurants; **Pizza Pizza Limited** (500 Kipling Avenue, Toronto, ON M8Z 5E5) is the operating company/franchisor; President & CEO Paul Goddard. — https://www.pizzapizza.ca/investors (a; opened 27 Sept 2026) and Q2 2026 results release (Section 4).
- **"Chicken Chicken" brand: could not be verified at tier (a)/(b) this session.** https://www.pizzapizza.ca/chicken-chicken rendered only a loading shell; https://www.chickenchicken.ca/ is an unrelated "COMING SOON" page; no Pizza Pizza Limited release exists on newswire.ca (the organisation page returns "No results found"); no mention in the Q1/Q2 2026 or Q3 2025 royalty releases. See Section 9.

### 1.5 A&W (Chubby Chicken line)
- **A&W Food Services of Canada Inc.** — publicly traded; boilerplate verbatim: "A&W is a publicly traded company and is the second largest quick-service hamburger restaurant chain in Canada. Operating coast-to-coast, A&W restaurants feature famous trade-marked menu items such as The Burger Family®, Chubby Chicken® and A&W Root Beer®." — Q2 FY2026 results, 23 July 2026, https://www.newswire.ca/news-releases/a-amp-w-food-services-of-canada-inc-announces-second-quarter-results-for-fiscal-2026-890757770.html (a; opened 27 Sept 2026). CEO Susan Senecal. Head office Vancouver, BC (dateline of 5 Jan 2026 release).

### 1.6 McDonald's Canada (Junior Chicken / Chicken Snack Wrap / McCrispy line)
- **McDonald's Restaurants of Canada Limited**. Verbatim boilerplate: "In 1967, Canada welcomed the first McDonald's restaurant to Richmond, British Columbia. Today, McDonald's Restaurants of Canada Limited has become part of the Canadian fabric, serving more than one million guests every day. In both franchised and corporate-owned restaurants, nearly 100,000 people are employed from coast to coast to coast, and more than 90 per cent of McDonald's 1,500 Canadian restaurants are locally owned and operated by independent franchisees. Of the almost $1 billion spent on food, more than 85 per cent is purchased from suppliers in Canada." — 13 Jan 2026 release, https://www.newswire.ca/news-releases/mcdonald-s-canada-announces-one-year-price-promise-on-5-mcvalue-r-meals-and-1-small-mccafe-r-coffee-864007730.html (a; opened 27 Sept 2026). President & CEO Annemarie Swijtink. Parent McDonald's Corporation (relationship not separately verified this session).

### 1.7 Wendy's Canada (chicken line)
- Verbatim from The Wendy's Company 10-K filed 23 Feb 2026 (a; https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/30697/000003069726000009/wen-20251228.htm, opened 27 Sept 2026): "Wendy's Restaurants of Canada Inc. ("WROC"), a 100% owned subsidiary of Wendy's, holds master franchise rights for Canada. The rights and obligations governing the majority of franchised restaurants operating in Canada are set forth in a Single Unit Sub-Franchise Agreement". Also: "The standard technical assistance fee is currently C$50,000 for each new restaurant opened." WROC office: Burlington, Ontario (leased, 8,917 sq ft). Wendy's/Tim Hortons combo units in Canada are leased via TimWen (10-K). Wendy's paid Canadian income taxes of $8,562 thousand (2025).
